Earlier: **Rebuilt the `endo_t2dm` Type 2 diabetes flowchart to the NEW NICE NG28 (February 2026) seven-track algorithm.** The user asked for detail on **second-line agent choice by comorbidity**, which the old chart lacked (its node was just "Add second agent" and the tip listed options without guiding the pick). Verified against NICE and two independent summaries that **NG28 was substantially updated on 18 February 2026** — this is AFTER the assistant's Jan 2026 knowledge cutoff, so the guidance was researched on the web rather than recalled. 7 nodes → **21 nodes**, `source` set to **"NICE NG28 (Feb 2026)"** (was unset, defaulting to "NICE").

**What changed clinically in the 2026 guideline (now reflected in the chart):**
- **Dual therapy is first-line for everyone**: metformin **modified-release** + an SGLT2 inhibitor, *even with no comorbidity* (previously metformin alone, with an SGLT2i added only for CVD/HF/high risk). M/R is now preferred over standard-release (equal efficacy, fewer GI effects, better adherence); standard-release retained for swallowing difficulties. Metformin 1 g/day if eGFR under 45, do not initiate below 30.
- **ASCVD gets TRIPLE therapy first-line**: metformin M/R + SGLT2i + **subcutaneous semaglutide up to 1 mg weekly**.
- **Early-onset (under 40)**: consider triple first-line with a GLP-1 RA or tirzepatide.
- **A seven-track algorithm** (no comorbidity / obesity / CKD / early-onset / heart failure / ASCVD / frailty), with instruction to agree priority if several apply.
- **CKD split by eGFR**: above 30 = standard dual; **20–30 = dapagliflozin or empagliflozin + DPP-4i**; under 20 = DPP-4i (then pioglitazone or insulin).
- **Frailty track**: metformin M/R, SGLT2i only if it will not cause harm (e.g. hypotension), otherwise DPP-4i.
- **Second-line is now comorbidity-driven** (the user's specific request): obesity or early-onset → **GLP-1 RA or tirzepatide**; newly developed ASCVD → **s/c semaglutide**; **no relevant comorbidity → DPP-4 inhibitor**; frailty → DPP-4i preferred. Then sulfonylurea (eGFR above 30) or pioglitazone (never in heart failure) or insulin.
- **Hard safety rule captured**: **never combine a GLP-1 RA or tirzepatide with a DPP-4 inhibitor.** Also: continue the SGLT2i for cardio-renal benefit even if it is not helping glycaemic control; stop a GLP-1 RA/tirzepatide if BMI falls below 18.5 or it is not achieving targets and not being given for CV benefit.
- Insulin: basal once/twice daily, adding prandial or pre-mixed if **HbA1c 75 mmol/mol or above**; symptomatic hyperglycaemia at any stage → insulin or sulfonylurea.
- Named GLP-1 RAs as of Feb 2026: liraglutide, dulaglutide, oral semaglutide (any licensed dose), s/c semaglutide (up to 1 mg).

**Also corrected the condition's `treatment` field**, which still read "Lifestyle then metformin; add SGLT2 inhibitor/GLP-1 **with** cardiovascular or renal disease" — the pre-2026 sequential model, which would have contradicted the new flowchart on the same card. No id changed and no condition was added or removed (total stays 523), so all RAG statuses/notes survive.

**Guide errors corrected silently in the notebook (recorded here per convention):**
- **p213 MEN 1 — wrong tumour.** The guide lists MEN 1 as "Pancreatic endocrine tumour (often **phaeochromocytoma**)". Phaeochromocytoma is a **MEN 2** feature; MEN 1 pancreatic tumours are **islet cell tumours (gastrinoma, insulinoma)**. Corrected, and the MEN1 vs RET genetics added.
- **p213 MEN 2B — wrong lesion.** The guide lists "**Acoustic Neuroma**". Acoustic (vestibular) schwannoma belongs to **neurofibromatosis type 2**; MEN 2B features **mucosal neuromas with intestinal ganglioneuromatosis** and a marfanoid habitus (with parathyroid disease characteristically absent). Corrected.
- **p220 alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency — wrong inheritance.** The guide says "**Autosomal dominant**". It is **autosomal recessive** (the PiZ/PiS alleles are co-dominantly expressed) — notable because the guide correctly flags the other two conditions in the same table as autosomal recessive "[HIGH YIELD]". Corrected.
- **p211 acute pancreatitis complications — wrong glucose direction.** The guide lists "shock, **hypoglycaemia** and hypocalcaemia". Islet destruction and the stress response cause **hyperglycaemia** (glucose above 10 is itself a Glasgow criterion). Corrected to hyperglycaemia; hypocalcaemia via calcium-soap fat necrosis retained.
- **p212 and p221 cholangiocarcinoma — conflated with gallbladder carcinoma.** The guide states cholangiocarcinoma is "90% associated with gallstones". That figure belongs to **gallbladder carcinoma**; cholangiocarcinoma's dominant risk factors are **primary sclerosing cholangitis, liver flukes, choledochal cysts and Lynch syndrome**. The two are now separated explicitly, with porcelain gallbladder flagged as premalignant.
- **p217 Child-Pugh survival figures don't match published data.** The guide gives A = 45%, B = 20%, C = under 20% "5-year survival". Those numbers correspond roughly to the conventional **1-year** figures for class C, not to class A at 5 years. Replaced with the standard 1-year and 2-year survivals (A ~100%/85%, B ~80%/60%, C ~45%/35%); the score components and class thresholds, which are sound, were kept verbatim.
- **p216 "Primary biliary cirrhosis"** — renamed **primary biliary cholangitis** in 2015 (the guide itself uses the newer name on p218, so it was internally inconsistent). Used consistently, with a note explaining the change.
- **p211 "GLASCOW Scale"** — spelling corrected to **Glasgow** (Glasgow-Imrie).

**Gap-filled from knowledge (guide silent or thin):** the **PANCREAS** components of the modified Glasgow score (the guide gives only the threshold of 3); that neither amylase nor lipase level reflects severity; **Courvoisier's law** stated as a rule rather than just a named sign; the five-Fs gallstone aide-memoire and why ultrasound (not plain film) is used given most stones are radiolucent; the **zone 1 viral vs zone 3 paracetamol/ischaemia** pairing; that loss of hepatic synthetic function explains hypoalbuminaemia and coagulopathy; **decompensation** features and 6-monthly HCC surveillance in cirrhosis; azathioprine as the steroid-sparing agent in autoimmune hepatitis; MEN1/RET genetics; and angiosarcoma's vinyl chloride, arsenic and thorotrast associations.

**Guide errors corrected silently in the notebook (recorded here per convention):**
- **p208 FAP genetics — wrong twice.** The guide states "70% AD mutation in APC tumour suppressor gene (C5q1), 30% AR mutation in DNA mismatch repair genes." FAP is **autosomal dominant APC disease on chromosome 5q21** (not "C5q1"); there is no 30% autosomal-recessive mismatch-repair form. The genuinely autosomal-recessive polyposis is **MUTYH-associated polyposis** (a **base-excision**-repair gene), while **mismatch-repair** genes cause **Lynch syndrome** — which the guide itself states correctly two paragraphs later, so it contradicts itself. Corrected and MUTYH named explicitly.
- **p206 Clostridioides difficile treatment — outdated.** The guide gives oral vancomycin and says metronidazole "can be used". Per **NICE NG199**, first line is **oral vancomycin 125 mg four times daily for 10 days**, second line (and for recurrence) **fidaxomicin**, and **metronidazole is no longer recommended** first or second line. Updated, and soap-and-water handwashing added (alcohol gel does not kill spores).
- **p203 coeliac serology — wrong order of preference.** The guide leads with anti-endomysial antibody as having "best sen and spec". Per **NICE NG20** the first-line test is **IgA tissue transglutaminase with a total IgA** (selective IgA deficiency is commoner in coeliac disease and causes false negatives), with **EMA second line**. Reordered, and the requirement to be eating gluten for at least six weeks added.
- **p203 villus-to-crypt ratio** — the guide says "~2:1"; the normal ratio is **3:1 to 5:1**. Corrected.
- **p208 adenoma malignancy risk** — the source text is garbled ("regular surveillance if over 3.4cm 45% malignant change"); rendered as an adenoma **larger than about 4 cm carrying a risk approaching 45%**, with size named as the dominant risk factor.
- **p209 "Duke's Staging"** — the A/B1/B2/C1/C2/D subdivision given is the **modified (Astler-Coller)** system, not classic Dukes (A/B/C/D); labelled as such, and the possessive corrected to Dukes.
- **p205–206 IBD maintenance** — the guide's layout implies aminosalicylates are used across both diseases. Clarified that **5-aminosalicylates are first line for maintaining remission in ulcerative colitis but of little value in Crohn's** (NICE NG129), and budesonide added as the steroid alternative for distal ileal/right-colonic Crohn's.
- **p205 ulcerative colitis cancer risk** — the flat "20-30x" figure is presented as "classically quoted as 20 to 30 times, **rising with the duration and extent of disease**", with colonoscopic surveillance noted, since the bare multiplier overstates modern risk.

**Gap-filled from knowledge (guide thin or silent):** why carcinoid **syndrome** requires hepatic metastases (portal drainage means serotonin is inactivated on hepatic first pass), plus chromogranin A, crisis precipitants and **carcinoid heart disease** (right-sided valve fibrosis, left side spared); the explicit **H. pylori triple-therapy regimen and 7-day duration** for peptic ulcer (the guide gave it only under MALT lymphoma) and that eradication alone can cause early MALT lymphoma to regress; **faecal calprotectin** in the IBD work-up; that a **gastric ulcer must always be biopsied** to exclude malignancy; Barrett's endoscopic surveillance; coeliac osteoporosis and the specific hyposplenism vaccinations; duodenal atresia's **double-bubble** sign and Down association; and **Hirschsprung-associated enterocolitis** as the life-threatening complication.

**Guide errors corrected silently in the notebook (recorded here per convention):**
- **p71 risk of malignancy index** — the guide scores the ultrasound component as "2" for two or more features, and menopausal status as "2" for postmenopausal. **Both are wrong**: RMI I uses **U = 3** for two or more features and **M = 3** for postmenopausal. Corrected.
- **p69 cervical brachytherapy** — the guide says rods of "radioactive **selenium**". Cervical brachytherapy uses **iridium-192** (selenium-75 is an industrial radiography source, not a clinical one). Corrected.
- **p66 ulipristal acetate** — the guide says use is "currently suspended due to a safety review". This is **outdated**: it is no longer part of routine fibroid practice and survives only as a restricted last-line option for women unsuitable for surgery or embolisation, with liver function monitoring. Updated.
- **p71 misfiled PACES box** — the "PACES TIPS" printed under the *Ovarian Cancer* heading actually describes **endometrial hyperplasia** ("abnormal thickening of the endometrium"; no atypia → LNG-IUS; atypia → hysterectomy + BSO). Moved into the endometrial hyperplasia topic where it belongs.
- **Typos** — p60 "**altex** condoms" → latex; p69 "+ lymphadenopath**y**" → lymphadenectomy; p68 "underlying **sin** condition" → skin; p71 "**Does** of carboplatin" → dose.
- **p59 bacterial vaginosis work-up** — the guide gives a bare list (vaginal pH, whiff test, Gram stain, HIV test, NAAT, VDRL) with no diagnostic criteria. Rewritten around the **Amsel criteria** and Hay-Ison/Nugent Gram-stain scoring, with the serology presented as part of a full sexual health screen rather than a bacterial vaginosis test.

Earlier: **rebuilt the STEMI branch of the Myocardial infarction (`cvs_mi`) management flowchart to full NICE NG185 detail.** The old chart collapsed the whole STEMI arm into one node — "Primary PCI within 120 min (else thrombolysis)" — with the fibrinolysis detail buried in a single tooltip. The STEMI arm is now a proper sub-pathway (`data.js` only): ST elevation/new LBBB → STEMI within 12 h → decision "Primary PCI within 120 min?" → **yes** = primary PCI + prasugrel/aspirin (clopidogrel if on an anticoagulant, ticagrelor/clopidogrel if 75+ or high bleeding risk); **no** = fibrinolysis (fibrin-specific agent e.g. tenecteplase) **+ antithrombin at the same time + a second antiplatelet** → repeat ECG at 60–90 min → "ST elevation resolved?" → **no (failed)** = immediate rescue PCI (never re-lyse); **yes** = inpatient angiography (pharmaco-invasive) → all → secondary prevention. NSTEMI arm (fondaparinux → GRACE) and the secondary-prevention node are unchanged; the old single "add 2nd antiplatelet" node was folded into the reperfusion/NSTEMI tips. ## Notebook (`notebook-data.js`) — partially written

19 sections (ids mirror the Tracker specialties). **ALL 19 SECTIONS ARE NOW WRITTEN — 271 topics, and no `_pending_*` placeholders remain anywhere.** The last one, `ent`, was written in Batch 16c(ii).

### 🗺️ Batch roadmap (authoritative — updated 2026-07-29)

**⚠️ REPRIORITISED 2026-07-29: the NEUROLOGY thread (Batches 20–23) runs NEXT, ahead of everything else. The Paediatrics thread then resumes at 16f exactly where it was left, followed by Psychiatry (19) and the two orphan Path Guide blocks (17, 18).** Nothing was renumbered — the neurology batches took the next free numbers, so 16f–16i keep their identity and page ranges.

**Why neurology jumped the queue — the numbers make the case.** The notebook's `neurology` section holds just **5 topics**, two of them barely started (`stroke_tia` at 944 characters, `headache_red_flags` at 1,180), against **34 Tracker conditions across 10 clinical clusters**. A cluster-by-cluster audit on 2026-07-29 found **26 of those 34 conditions have no notebook coverage at all** — including the whole of epilepsy, the dementias, movement disorders, neuromuscular disease, peripheral nerve lesions and the spinal cord syndromes. It is by some distance the largest remaining gap between what the Tracker asks the user to revise and what the Notebook actually teaches.

**⚠️ Scoping fact that changes how these batches must be written: the guides only cover about HALF of neurology, so these are NOT pure extraction batches.** The Specialities Guide has **no adult neurology section whatsoever** (it runs O&G → Paediatrics → Psychiatry). The Path Guide contributes four blocks and no more — **Cerebral Pathology p244–248**, **Neurodegenerative Diseases p249–251**, **CNS Infection and Meningitis p56–57**, and **Prion Disease p82**. Everything else — headache, epilepsy, multiple sclerosis, myasthenia, Guillain-Barré, the peripheral nerve lesions, the spinal cord syndromes — has **no source in either guide and must be written from NICE and specialty guidance, web-verified before writing**. Budget for that: these batches are closer in character to the paediatric ones (where the guide was management-only and needed heavy gap-filling) than to the GI or endocrine pathology batches.

The earlier **2026-07-26 reprioritisation** (bring adult gastrointestinal and adult endocrinology forward, ahead of the Paediatrics/Psychiatry threads) is complete — Batches 12–15 are all done.

**How the neurology thread was scoped (2026-07-29).** The four batches are cut to mirror the Tracker's own clinical clusters, so that finishing them leaves every Tracker neurology condition with somewhere to read about it. Mapping of the 26 uncovered conditions: **Batch 20** takes *Cerebrovascular disease* (subdural haematoma was the only wholly uncovered one, but stroke and TIA both need real content rather than the current stub); **Batch 21** takes *Dementias* and *Movement disorders & neurodegeneration* in full (Alzheimer's, vascular, frontotemporal, Lewy body, Parkinson's, PSP, MSA, motor neurone disease, Friedreich's ataxia); **Batch 22** takes *Epilepsy & seizures* in full plus the three uncovered *Headache & facial pain* entries (cluster, trigeminal neuralgia, idiopathic intracranial hypertension); **Batch 23** takes *Demyelinating & neuromuscular*, *Peripheral nerve disorders* and *Spinal cord & autonomic disorders* in full. Expect roughly **7–8 topics per batch, taking `neurology` from 5 to about 30** — proportionate to its 34 Tracker conditions, and bringing it into line with `gastro` (21) and `reproductive` (37).

**Two conditions are deliberately NOT in the neurology batches.** **Cerebral palsy** is paediatric and is left for **16h** (Paediatrics — Musculoskeletal + Neurological), which already covers that page range. **Wernicke's encephalopathy** is already substantially covered inside `alcohol_misuse_dependence` in `psychiatry`; Batch 21 should cross-reference it rather than duplicate it, and only write a standalone topic if that proves too thin.

**One genuinely new destination:** Batch 23 writes **adult bacterial meningitis into `infectious`**, which currently has no meningitis topic at all despite `id_meningitis` existing in the Tracker and a full paediatric version having been written in Batch 16e. The NG240 research from that batch is recorded in this file and can be reused directly.

**The Batch 13 cross-batch deferral is now CLOSED.** The diabetes mellitus block held back from Path Guide p211 (diagnostic thresholds, T1 versus T2 mechanism, the macro/microvascular complications table) was written into `diabetes_overview` in Batch 15, so diabetes now lives in one place. No deferrals are currently outstanding.

**Paediatrics block map (established 2026-07-27, use this to scope 16b–16i).** The Paediatrics section has its own contents page at **p75** and runs p78–200 in 17 named blocks: Neonatal Medicine 78–86, Cardiac Disease 87–92, Allergy & Respiratory 93–114, Gastroenterology 115–127, Liver Disorders 128–130, Infection & Immunity 131–140, Dermatology 141–154, Kidneys & Urinary Tract 155–160, Genitalia 161–163, Malignant Disease 164–166, Haematological Disease 167–170, Musculoskeletal 171–173, Neurological 174–184, Endocrinological 185–190, Paediatric Emergencies 191–195, Ophthalmological 196, Other Topics 197–200. **The block headings are inline, not at the top of a page**, so locate them by regex on a standalone line rather than by reading page tops; Genitalia, Haematological, Neurological and Ophthalmological have no matchable standalone heading and must be anchored on their first condition (Epididymo-Orchitis p161, Beta Thalassaemia p167, Attention Deficit p174, Hypermetropia p196).

**The paediatrics section of the guide is a MANAGEMENT guide only.** It gives treatment with almost no definition, presentation, investigation or pathology, so every topic needs heavy gap-filling — budget for that. It also overlaps heavily with the already-written O&G thread (`torch_infections`, `pregnancy_uti_gbs`, `antenatal_care`) and with `genetics`, `cardiology`, `respiratory` and `infectious`: **check for an existing owner before writing, and carry only the paediatric-specific material with a cross-reference.**

**Ordering within `paediatrics`:** the neonatal block was placed **first** and the two pre-existing topics (`paediatric_hip_conditions`, `developmental_milestones`) moved to the end, so that 16b–16i can slot their system blocks in between and the section ends up in the guide's own order. Hip is MSK and milestones belong with Neurological, which is where they will sit once 16h lands.

**Precedent set in Batch 15 — a batch may write outside its nominal target section.** The Path Guide's Chemical Pathology chapter is not all endocrine, and three blocks were placed by subject rather than by chapter: renal stones → `renal`, nutrition → `gastro`, screening statistics → `public_health`. Apply the same test in later batches: put the topic where the user will look for it, and cross-reference, rather than keeping a chapter artificially intact.

**Batches 17 and 18 exist because two Chemical Pathology page blocks were orphans.** Auditing the page ranges either side of Batch 14 found two runs that no batch covered, both because the roadmap's endocrine ranges were written as "p103–110" and "p112–116, 119–127":

- **p111 "Therapeutic Drug Monitoring"** sits between the adrenal and calcium sections. It is pharmacology, not endocrine, and `pharmacology` is the thinnest written notebook section (1 topic) — hence Batch 17.
- **p117–118 "Enzymes and Cardiac Markers"** sits between the calcium and lipoprotein sections. It is a coherent standalone block that spans three specialties: cardiac markers (BNP, NT-proBNP, troponin, CK-MB) point at `cardiology`, amylase and lipase at `gastro` (already covered in `pancreatitis_pathology`, so cross-reference rather than repeat), and ALP at `calcium_homeostasis` (which already carries the ALP column of the bone-biochemistry table). **Proposed destination `cardiology` with cross-references both ways — worth confirming with the user before writing.**

Neither was forced into `endocrinology` just to keep the page numbers contiguous. Note also that **p117 carries a stray "Hypercalcaemia" sub-heading** immediately under "Enzymes and Cardiac Markers" with no hypercalcaemia content beneath it — a layout artefact in the guide, not a missed section (hypercalcaemia is genuinely covered at p112–116 and is already written up in `calcium_homeostasis`).

**Guide extraction project (started 2026-07-10):** systematically distilling the two MedEd PDFs (Path Guide + Specialities Guide, in the parent folder) into notebook topics, in batches, thinnest/highest-yield sections first — correcting errors and updating outdated guidance as we go. **Convention (user preference, 2026-07-10): do NOT put "the guide was wrong / guideline-update" notes inside notebook topics.** The notebook must read as clean, correct content with the corrected facts stated plainly; the record of what the guide got wrong lives ONLY here in this log (so the user can ask later). The three in-notebook source/update notes originally added in Batches 1–3 (sickle-cell withdrawn drugs, VTE first-line, CLL/CML mislabel) were removed on 2026-07-10 — the corrected facts remain in the topics, and the errors themselves are still documented in the batch entries below. **Batch 1 (Haematology, anaemia cluster, from the Path Guide p7–17):** added `anaemia_overview` (MCV framework + iron-studies table + IDA/ACD/sideroblastic), `macrocytic_anaemia` (B12/folate, pernicious anaemia, replace-B12-before-folate rule), `haemolytic_anaemias` (intra/extravascular, hereditary spherocytosis, G6PD, warm/cold AIHA, PNH; cross-refs Thrombocytopenia for MAHA), `haemoglobinopathies` (sickle cell + thalassaemia). **Corrections/updates:** flagged that the guide's crizanlizumab and voxelotor are both withdrawn (crizanlizumab EU 2023; voxelotor worldwide Sept 2024) and pointed to CRISPR gene therapy (Casgevy); modernised iron chelation to include oral agents (deferasirox/deferiprone). Haematology notebook 1→5 topics. **Batch 2 (Haematology, haemostasis cluster, Path Guide p7 + p18–25):** added `blood_films` (peripheral blood film reference table), `bleeding_disorders` (coagulation cascade, platelet vs coagulation pattern, vascular defects, haemophilia A/B, von Willebrand, vitamin K/liver, clotting-screen table; cross-refs Thrombocytopenia for DIC), `vte_thrombophilia` (Virchow, Wells, inherited/acquired thrombophilia table, antiphospholipid), `anticoagulants` (heparin/warfarin/DOAC mechanisms, target-INR + high-INR + reversal tables). **Corrections/updates:** updated VTE treatment to NICE NG158 (DOAC first-line; unprovoked VTE anticoagulated long-term, not a fixed 6 months); noted warfarin preferred over DOAC in antiphospholipid syndrome; fixed the guide's "idracizumab" → idarucizumab. Haematology notebook 5→9 topics. **Batch 3 (Haematology, malignant, Path Guide p26–39) — completes the Haematology section:** added `leukaemias` (ALL/AML/CML/CLL comparison, Auer rods/APML/Philadelphia/smear cells, tumour lysis, allo vs auto SCT), `lymphoma` (Hodgkin Reed-Sternberg/ABVD vs NHL Burkitt/DLBCL/mantle/follicular/MALT + translocation list), `myeloma` (CRAB, Bence Jones, MGUS/smouldering/MM table, Waldenström's, AL amyloid), `mds_aplastic` (MDS dysplasia/IPSS, aplastic anaemia). **Corrections/updates:** flagged the guide's mislabelling of CML's chronic/accelerated/blast phases + imatinib under a "CLL" heading (they are CML; CLL is staged by Binet); noted daratumumab increasingly first-line in myeloma and eltrombopag added to aplastic-anaemia immunosuppression. Haematology notebook 9→13 topics — **Haematology section now complete.** **Batch 4 (electrolytes & acid–base, Path Guide chemical pathology p88–98):** added `sodium_disorders`, `potassium_disorders`, `acid_base` to **Renal** (electrolyte/acid–base block) and `siadh_di` to **Endocrinology** (the ADH pair — best-fit placement; cross-referenced from sodium_disorders). Sodium (osmolality-then-volume framework, pseudo/true, central pontine myelinolysis correction rule, hypernatraemia); potassium (causes/ECG/treatment, Conn's-vs-Addison's pattern); acid–base (ABG framework, anion gap KUL-T, osmolar gap); SIADH criteria + diabetes insipidus (cranial vs nephrogenic, water deprivation test). **Corrections/updates (kept out of the notebook per convention):** modernised hyperkalaemia gut binders to sodium zirconium cyclosilicate/patiromer (guide said calcium/sodium polystyrene sulfonate); used the standard anion-gap formula Na−(Cl+HCO3), normal ~4–12 (guide used the K-inclusive Na+K−Cl−HCO3, 14–18); framed SIADH resistant-case treatment around vaptans/tolvaptan (guide led with demeclocycline). Renal notebook 2→5, Endocrinology 3→4. **Batch 5 (Obstetrics & Gynaecology, Specialities Guide p24–58) — into the Reproductive section:** added `contraception_overview` (LARC + short-acting + emergency contraception, UKMEC, missed-pill rules), `menopause_hrt` (HRT types/routes/risks/contraindications, non-hormonal, perimenopausal contraception), `pregnancy_hypertension` (chronic/gestational HTN, pre-eclampsia + aspirin prophylaxis, eclampsia magnesium regimen, HELLP), `gestational_diabetes` (OGTT screening, diagnostic thresholds, targets, stepwise Rx, postnatal). This is the first of several O&G batches (the source spans p6–72). **Corrections/updates (kept out of the notebook):** Mirena/LNG-IUS contraceptive licence now up to 8 years (guide said 3–5); stated the explicit NICE gestational-diabetes diagnostic thresholds (fasting ≥5.6 or 2-hour ≥7.8 mmol/L); noted transdermal HRT carries lower VTE risk. Reproductive notebook 4→8. **Batch 6 (Obstetrics & Gynaecology — early pregnancy + PCOS/subfertility, Specialities Guide p46–58) — into the Reproductive section:** added `miscarriage` (types table threatened/inevitable/incomplete/complete/missed/septic; TVUSS diagnostic thresholds crown–rump length ≥7 mm or mean sac diameter ≥25 mm with no heartbeat; expectant [7–14 days] vs medical [misoprostol; mifepristone first for a *missed* miscarriage] vs surgical [manual vacuum aspiration/evacuation]; anti-D for surgical management only; recurrent-miscarriage workup), `ectopic_pregnancy` (95% tubal; hCG discriminatory zone ~1500 IU/L and the ≥63%/48 h rise for a viable intrauterine pregnancy; expectant vs methotrexate vs surgical criteria table by size/hCG/symptoms; salpingectomy first-line vs salpingotomy if infertility risk; anti-D, no Kleihauer), `gestational_trophoblastic_disease` (complete [diploid, wholly paternal, usually 46,XX, ~15% malignant] vs partial [triploid 69,XXX/XXY, ~0.5%] mole; snowstorm ultrasound; suction curettage; trophoblastic screening-centre follow-up; gestational trophoblastic neoplasia/choriocarcinoma → early lung metastases → highly-curable chemotherapy), `pcos` (Rotterdam two-of-three; supportive LH:FSH/testosterone/SHBG; unopposed-oestrogen endometrial-hyperplasia/cancer risk; management tailored to the woman's menstrual/hirsutism/fertility/metabolic concern), `subfertility` (definition + when to investigate early; mid-luteal day-21 progesterone; anti-Müllerian hormone + antral follicle count for ovarian reserve; semen analysis; tubal patency by hysterosalpingography/HyCoSy/laparoscopy-and-dye; ovulation induction → intrauterine insemination → IVF/ICSI/donor gametes). **Corrections/updates (kept out of the notebook per convention):** stated **letrozole as first-line ovulation induction in PCOS** (guide gave only clomifene; letrozole is now preferred per the international evidence-based PCOS guideline and increasingly NICE); for a *missed* miscarriage used **mifepristone then misoprostol** (guide gave misoprostol alone; reflects the updated NICE NG126 after the MifeMiso trial), with misoprostol alone for an incomplete miscarriage; standardised methotrexate counselling to **avoid conception for at least 3 months** (the guide says 3 months on p47 but 6 on p48); clarified **anti-D applies to surgical miscarriage management only**, not a threatened or medical loss before 12 weeks; gap-filled the full miscarriage classification, the complete-vs-partial mole karyotypes/malignant potential, and choriocarcinoma pulmonary spread (guide thin on all three). Note: **gestational trophoblastic disease and subfertility have no Tracker condition** (`repro_pcos`/`repro_ectopic`/`repro_miscarriage` already exist) — candidates if the Tracker is ever extended. Reproductive notebook 8→13; **notebook total 77→82**. **Batch 7 (Cardiac pathology, Path Guide p187–194) — into the Cardiology section; the first Path Guide batch since the haematology run, following the user's request to bring cardiac + lung pathology forward:** added 6 pathology topics that complement the existing clinical overviews (`acs_overview`/`af_overview`/`heart_failure_overview`): `atherosclerosis_ihd` (atherogenesis cascade, stable/unstable/Prinzmetal angina, MI pathogenesis, the histological evolution timeline, and MI complications — mechanical/arrhythmic/pericardial/thrombotic, including Dressler's, ventricular aneurysm, and papillary-muscle/free-wall/septal rupture; cross-refs `acs_overview` for management), `cardiomyopathies` (dilated/hypertrophic/restrictive mechanism table + ARVC; HCM autosomal-dominant sarcomeric mutations, beta-MHC, myocyte disarray, sudden death, HOCM), `valvular_heart_disease` (AS/AR/MS/MR murmur-and-cause table, mitral valve prolapse, chronic rheumatic valve pattern mitral>aortic>tricuspid>pulmonary), `rheumatic_fever` (Jones criteria table, group A strep antigenic mimicry, Aschoff bodies/Anitschkov myocytes, benzylpenicillin + prophylaxis, chronic rheumatic heart disease), `infective_endocarditis` (acute S. aureus vs subacute viridans/HACEK, IVDU→tricuspid, Osler/Roth/Janeway/splinter signs, Duke criteria, three sets of cultures + echo, treatment; plus the sterile-vegetation differential — marantic and Libman–Sacks), `pericardial_disease` (pericarditis causes/saddle-ST ECG + colchicine, effusion, tamponade with Beck's triad → pericardiocentesis, constrictive pericarditis). Cardiology notebook 3→9; **notebook total 82→88.** **Notes (kept out of the notebook per convention):** the Path Guide is a pathology source and was accurate for this section (no factual errors flagged); where it omits management I added guideline-level detail (colchicine for pericarditis, Duke-criteria antibiotic choices, aspirin-free ACS secondary prevention via the cross-reference). **Batch 8 (Lung pathology, Path Guide p195–200) — into the Respiratory section, completing the reprioritised cardiac+lung pathology pair:** added 4 pathology topics and augmented `restrictive_lung_disease`, deliberately including EVERY fact from the guide's lung-pathology section (the user's explicit request) while avoiding duplication with the existing clinical/spirometry topics. New topics: `obstructive_lung_disease_pathology` (the five-disease comparison table — chronic bronchitis / bronchiectasis / asthma / emphysema / small-airway disease, with site, pathology, aetiology and histology including Curschmann spirals + Charcot-Leyden crystals; the bronchiectasis vicious cycle and full cause list including Young's syndrome and primary ciliary dyskinesia; cystic fibrosis CFTR/F508del/Pseudomonas), `pneumonia_patterns` (bronchopneumonia vs lobar — the four hepatisation stages — vs atypical), `lung_cancer` (SCC/adeno/small-cell/large-cell types table, paraneoplastic table including PTHrP/SIADH/Cushing's/Lambert-Eaton/carcinoid, molecular markers EGFR/KRAS/EML4-ALK/ERCC1, TNM staging, mesothelioma cross-referenced to `asbestos_lung_disease`), `pulmonary_vascular_disease` (pulmonary embolism 95%-from-DVT/Virchow/saddle embolus/non-thrombotic emboli, pulmonary hypertension WHO classes 1–5 + pre/capillary/post-capillary mechanism, pulmonary oedema heart-failure-cells/batwing/Kerley B lines, diffuse alveolar damage → ARDS and neonatal hyaline membrane disease). Augmented `restrictive_lung_disease` (id unchanged, bookmarks safe) with the guide's remaining ILD detail it lacked: the granuloma definition + infectious/non-infectious granulomatous causes, the organising-pneumonia/BOOP histology of extrinsic allergic alveolitis, and cheese-washer's lung. Respiratory notebook 5→9; **notebook total 88→92.** **Notes:** the Path Guide is a pathology source and was accurate here; the existing `restrictive_lung_disease` and `asbestos_lung_disease` topics already covered ILD/IPF/pneumoconiosis/sarcoidosis/mesothelioma, so those were cross-referenced rather than repeated; a source-level completeness check confirmed all 52 sampled guide facts are present. **Batch 9 (Obstetrics core — antenatal care, labour & obstetric emergencies, Specialities Guide p8–43) — into the Reproductive section:** added 6 topics resuming the O&G thread: `antenatal_care` (Naegele's rule/EDD, the 10-vs-7 visit schedule, booking bloods + infection screening, folic acid 400mcg/5mg + vitamin D, the combined 11–14wk vs quadruple 15–20wk Down's screening, dating/anomaly scans, the anti-D schedule at 28 & 34 weeks, SFH from 24wk, flu/pertussis vaccines), `labour` (the three stages with durations, mechanism of labour, active vs physiological third-stage management with 10 units oxytocin, preterm labour — betamethasone/nifedipine tocolysis/magnesium neuroprotection/vaginal progesterone/cerclage, PROM vs P-PROM with erythromycin), `induction_of_labour` (indications, the Bishop score, methods — membrane sweep → vaginal PGE2/dinoprostone or balloon catheter → ARM → oxytocin, hyperstimulation, VBAC 72–75% with 1-in-200 rupture risk), `cardiotocography` (DR C BRAVADO, the deceleration types — early benign/variable cord/late hypoxia, sinusoidal, and the normal/suspicious/pathological classification), `antepartum_haemorrhage` (placenta praevia vs placental abruption comparison table — painless/soft vs painful/woody-hard, the never-do-a-VE rule, management of each), `obstetric_emergencies` (shoulder dystocia drill — McRoberts/suprapubic/Rubin/Wood's/posterior arm, cord prolapse, uterine rupture, and PPH by the four Ts). Pre-eclampsia/eclampsia and gestational diabetes were already covered (`pregnancy_hypertension`, `gestational_diabetes`), so cross-referenced not repeated. Reproductive notebook 13→19; **notebook total 92→98.** **Batch 10 (Maternal infection + liver disease in pregnancy, Specialities Guide p28–39) — completes the O&G obstetrics thread:** added 4 topics: `torch_infections` (toxoplasmosis triad, congenital rubella syndrome + MMR pre-pregnancy, CMV as commonest congenital infection/deafness, neonatal HSV → elective CS for 3rd-trimester primary, and the "Other" — syphilis/benzylpenicillin, parvovirus B19 → fetal anaemia/hydrops → intrauterine transfusion, listeria/amoxicillin), `chickenpox_pregnancy` (immunity check, VZIG prophylaxis for non-immune exposure vs aciclovir once rash appears, congenital varicella syndrome, peripartum neonatal varicella), `pregnancy_uti_gbs` (asymptomatic bacteriuria — nitrofurantoin but avoid at term, trimethoprim avoided 1st trimester; GBS risk-based intrapartum benzylpenicillin; HIV — ART for all + delivery by 36-wk viral load 50 copies/mL threshold + no breastfeeding; hepatitis B neonatal Ig+vaccine/breastfeeding safe, hepatitis C defer treatment; maternal Sepsis Six), `pregnancy_liver_disease` (obstetric cholestasis — palm/sole itch no rash, bile acids, stillbirth risk driving timed delivery, ursodeoxycholic acid; acute fatty liver — hypoglycaemia/coagulopathy/LCHAD, urgent delivery; HELLP cross-referenced to pre-eclampsia). Reproductive notebook 19→23; **notebook total 98→102.** **Roadmap: the O&G obstetrics thread (Batches 5–6, 9–10) is DONE. ## CPS question bank (`question-bank-data.js`) — seed set

**43 single-best-answer questions** across all 19 sections (Genetics 6, Immunology & Allergy 3; others 1–3 each). Comfortable seed for practice; expand any section by appending to `QBANK.questions` with a unique `id` and the matching `section` tag.

## Professional Dilemmas (`dilemmas-data.js`) — seed set

**13 scenarios**: 9 ranking + 4 multiple-best-answer, spread across the three themes — Professional Integrity (5), Coping with Pressure (4), Empathy & Sensitivity (4). Expand by appending to `DILEMMAS.scenarios`.

## Suggested next content jobs

1. Write the last scaffolded notebook section (`ent`). Several written sections have only 1-2 topics and could be expanded (e.g. dermatology beyond skin cancer; MSK beyond fractures/vasculitides).
2. Grow the CPS bank toward ~10 questions per section.
3. Add more Professional Dilemmas scenarios — aim for an even spread across the three themes and both formats.
